The second point I wanted to take up is this very Austrian system of on-the-spot checks, which could actually be made by the FMA, but which is being carried out in Austria by the National Bank. That's a little Austrian solution. This does not sound particularly bad at the first moment, but of course has consequences and consequences. Two of these beauty errors are for me that the National Bank actually belongs to the Republic, that is, to us taxpayers, and that is why we are rightly financed and, so to speak, paid. But the National Bank takes over these tasks from the FMA, and one could say: This is paid for anyway; there is a budget that the FMA sends to the National Bank to carry it out. – That’s right, but it’s far too little! In fact, only EUR 10 million will be paid, but it costs about EUR 50 million per year. This means that we pay taxpayers for a second time and pay on it. The second mistake of beauty – which is also regularly addressed by the Court of Auditors – is that there are double structures and double-tracks. In terms of efficiency, of course, this is also something we would have liked to solve; and now I come back with the commercial bank Mattersburg: Yes, of course, that was a system failure because it was a criminal case, that's quite clear. However, I would like to say – and this is also true of many experts who have been observing this for years – that it would be better, of course, if one had clear responsibilities. If the right hand does not know what the left hand is doing, and if communication between the authorities takes some months – well, then one will not be able to solve such a crime. So what would you do? – Eliminate double tracks, look at the dosages and set up them properly, and so, as it is, keep the FMA really independent and guarantee it in the future. If we did this in the future, we would be even happier, but as I said: We like the package as a whole and will also give it our consent. Thank you.

However, this is only possible if the relevant provisions are in place. There are different conditions for this. First of all, it is important that there are no practical obstacles; then it is important that the financial obstacles are reduced; and above all, it is important that efficient structures are present. Only if these three conditions are met can we actually speak of easy access to justice. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] This innovation brings us very, very great progress in all these points. There is a great relief in the digital field: in the field of digital file management, on the one hand, but also in the field of external access to these digital files, one can also make digital copies at a price appropriate to the digital age, namely, according to data volume and no longer by page. That sounds like a banality, but actually, if you count it down on cent and euro, it means a very big leap. Especially in the field of digitalization, we have been able to take away a lot of what we learned in two years' time, because it was important that digital access to the right is there and can be used. That is why we were able to test the rules that we are now going to implement in the field, so to speak. Some of them have proved themselves very well, others have come up with them: No, we do not want them better, because they do not facilitate access to the law, but are uncertain of the results of the case law! That is why we have included in this novel those provisions and regulations that facilitate access to the law and help us to make the whole process more efficient, cost-effective and down-to-earth. We have not taken into account those who are insecure in the area of procedural management, in the area of the immediacy of case law, for example, the video negotiations in which many issues have arisen. That's what we said: Well, we tried it, it was an attempt, it was necessary in the pandemic, but it is not suitable for permanent law, and therefore we do not make it out of a very conscious decision! Another point, and it is also very important to me, regarding financial access to justice, are the court fees. These represent a high threshold for many people, and it is very important that the fees are so high that they keep pace with the circumstances that have just existed. We are currently experiencing a massive inflation – we have already discussed this very intensively in the morning – and that is why the increase in court fees is suspended. This too is a very important point in facilitating access to justice for people. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Let me say one last point, as we are in the process of reducing barriers: In cases where the proceedings are under the Law on Equal Opportunities, access to the Supreme Court shall be allowed for ten years for all proceedings, regardless of the level of the dispute. This is a very important area in which decisions can then be taken on which later case law can be based. There again, access to justice is facilitated. I remember that our civil process order was at the heart of the end of the 19th century. ==References== That was an extremely advanced law at the time. It was then introduced the free evidence of the judges. Previously, there were fixed rules of evidence to be adhered to. It was then entrusted to the judges to establish, on the basis of the evidence available, that: What was it? What is the matter of decision-making? Now we have gone on for many decades, have gained many scientific findings, especially in the field of psychology, and know that this is sometimes a very difficult, yes, I want to say, hopeless undertaking, because the truth is something – and this can probably confirm to anyone who works in practice – which is not always objectively the truth. I know from parties who say the truth subjectively for themselves, however, expert advice or other evidence that it was not the objective truth. To solve conflicts in this field of tension does not always lead to desired results. That is why it would be very desirable for us to continue to develop the civil process system. ParlaMint-AT_2022-03-23-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00147_d7e14990	We're on our way to the 11th. Agenda item. I welcome Mr Martin Polashek to the House. There was no oral reporting. The first speaker is Mr Rudolf Taschner. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-03-23-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00147_d7e15020	Madam President. High house! Apotropaion: This wonderfully sounding foreign word means sorcerers, something that, like a miracle, turns everything to the best. We are always looking for an apotropaion, and in fact some, even many, believe to have found this miraculous means in digitization. Digitalization in all areas of life will make the world better, especially digitization in education and teaching, according to faith. In education and teaching, however, there has been another apotropaion for years, which is the word competence. Everything must be competent, with competence we will achieve a good school. Now, of course, it is obvious to mix these two magic agents into one: We want to communicate digital skills. So the school would have really gotten better. You may see my quiet scepticism in these words, but, ladies and gentlemen, I do not want to be misunderstandingd. Digitalisation is really extremely important in teaching, and it will mean a great deal of progress. We also know that digitization has been an essential tool in Corona's time. It is very crucial that we give digitalisation a real priority. In two ways, I want to see this in class: In one respect, it is that: digitisation improves teaching in traditional subjects. When you learn vocabulary, when presenting chemical reactions on the computer – if you actually see the molecules before you see how they come together to form new molecules – and in many other areas of digitization in traditional teaching, you can give a wide space. On the other hand, it is essential that we spread the basics and knowledge of digitization – that is, this field of digital basic education – as far as possible, as far as possible, as far as digitization can do, but also where digitization – and that too must be disseminated – can lead to error and what a computer simply cannot do. A computer cannot dream – there are also things that the computer does not control, and that also needs to be known. In this respect, the need for digitalisation is very important and it is important that we can support this in this proposal through financial collateral. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen, digital competence will not be the real means of miracle. If you ask me what the apotropaion is in school, I can give you the answer: These are the personalities of teachers who know, first of all, that you must take seriously the personality of each individual child, of every individual young person you face, and the second of them are enthusiastic about their subject and can pass on this enthusiasm and knowledge in their subject. That's what it's all about. Many of these teachers teach at Austrian schools, of course we want more, so that the true apotropaion that will lead to the better school really works. Digitalization is only an aid. Thank you.

In Austria, on average, 5 percent of all pupils require special education. What is striking is the variation in the federal states. In the school year 2019/2020, for example, 7.2 percent of all pupils in Vorarlberg were identified with a special educational need, whereas in Tyrol it was only 3.4 percent. There are also differences in the school form visited. For example, in Carinthia, almost 85 percent of pupils are taught with SPF, while in Vienna it is not even 50 percent. I don't think that's the case. It is probably due to the SPF’s award practice in the federal states and thus the SPF-Quote varies. In order to answer all these questions and really achieve a uniform system in Austria, this motion for a resolution is available because the findings on how the SPF allocation is to be replaced and in which settings the SPF resources then flow are necessary in order to create more transparency in the allocation of resources and to achieve an optimization of the distribution. He knew that the climate is controlled by a chaotic, dynamic nonlinear system – as the IPCC says – that predictions are very, very difficult to make if they are possible at all. The mathematics of the chaotic systems is a very complicated matter. Böhm, however, has actually examined the climate with a precision and in a clarity that seeks the same. There are a number of charlatans in climateology, I could name some. Nomina sunt odiosa – if you ask me, I can tell her, but here I don't want to call her. The new institution, which is now being formed, may host capacities and researchers, just as Reinhard Böhm has been one to continue this tradition. I think that's something I can wish for.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-03-23-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00147_d7e19050	The Federal Minister has spoken. I can give him the word. Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-03-23-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00147_d7e19071	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Before I go into the matter, I may point out something: It's a nice coincidence, but, I think, it's quite good that we're dealing with this topic today, on the World Meteorology Day. This is a special day that reminds us that on a 23rd day March the Convention of the World Organization for Meteorology was launched by the UN, which at the time was concerned with ensuring the networking of weather data and truly promoting meteorological research as a global project. That's right, I think, today quite well, because this law makes it its own geosphere Austria, in short: GSA, created, a joint, fusion geological, geophysical and meteorological service. Through the combination of two institutions, we make a truly sustainable contribution to securing the life and economic bases of Austria, to a knowledge-based approach to climate change and its consequences, to sustainable development in Austria and also to increasing the state-wide resistance and crisis stability. Such a central authority becomes increasingly important especially in times of climate change and thus associated exceptional weather events and extreme weather conditions. You know the current urgent requirements as well as we do: It is about spatial planning concepts, taking particular account of the underground. It is about protecting the settlement and economic area as well as the infrastructure from natural hazards. It is also about sustainable and environmentally sustainable raw material generation and energy storage, the safeguarding of groundwater reserves and, last but not least, the use of alternative energy forms such as geothermal, solar or wind energy. On current occasions, I can also remind you that the services are also part of the field of radiation protection. This means that appropriate simulations of the spread of pollutant clouds are carried out to ensure the safety of the population. The Geosphere Austria is now setting up a joint institution with almost 500 employees in the future. This is a research organisation that is truly committed to scientific integrity. This is a central competence of the Federal Government for data and information on the entire geosphere, i.e. the atmosphere, the lithosphere, the hydrosphere and also the pedosphere. This will be an institution of public law with its own legal personality, financed by a three-year service agreement with the federal government, with an annual budget of almost 40 million euros. It has already been mentioned: Yes, the geosphere Austria can also provide services against charges. I would like to stress that this has already been the case at the Central Institute for Meteorology and Geodynamics as well as at the Geological Bundesanstalt. They have already been on the market as partial bodies. I see nothing bad in that, on the contrary: If we were to privatize such services with a stroke now, the know-how that exists in these institutions with which quality assurance works would suddenly be lost. We would be subject to uncontrolled privatisation. What the opposition demands would mean that 250 people would lose their jobs. We don't want to. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] There is no additional cost, there is no distortion of competition, but there is a meaningful and innovative step towards the union of two. single institutions so far. The aim is to avoid double-tracking, and in the future it will be to link the entire data volumes of both institutions in order to ensure exactly what has already been addressed several times: to provide all these data in a transparent way in the sense of open data, to provide and to combine data beyond the two previous institutions, to research institutions, but of course also to the public, to work with these data themselves. So this is a very, very important step towards Open Science. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] Because it has been addressed: This new institution is headed by a scientific and commercial Director-General, a Director-General appointed for a period of five years and, in particular, by senior officials. The order will, of course, be made transparent. This independent management, on the one hand, and a service agreement with the Ministry, on the other, gives this institution the greatest possible autonomy and, at the same time, a financial security and a strong organisational framework. A board of trustees will be set up, with ten members from different ministries, all above all from the Ministry of Education, but also from the staff representation, in order to ensure maximum transparency. And a scientific advisory board consisting of nine members will be set up, seven will be proposed by the Scientific Directorate-General and two members of the Austrian University Conference. Since it is about the employees: All new employees are employed in accordance with the employee law. The officials who have been employed so far will be established, just as has been done in connection with the autonomy of the universities, in their own office, the geosphere Austria. For contract staff, the contract staff law remains the service-based basis. I can stress that it was important to me that there was neither deterioration for the federal staff nor for employees within the framework of partial justice. I can assure you that last week I was at the Central Institute for Meteorology and Geodynamics, I was at the Geological Federal Institute this Monday and had the opportunity to talk to many people who work there in the administration and in science and application. What I took with me: There is an extreme mood of departure, they are highly motivated and they are delighted to be able to contribute to our country together in a new institution. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] The preparatory measures for the merger must now be put in place so that we can start as quickly as possible and then implement them in order to continue to guarantee the important achievements of the two institutions so that we can continue to be well equipped for the requirements in the future. I may return to the World Meteorology Day: The motto here is Early Warning, Early Action, so early warning systems for quick action. We need this facility, and these institutions will become all the more powerful in the new geosphere Austria and will be able to cooperate better with other services worldwide, but also with universities and other research institutions and continue to fulfil these important state tasks for us. That is why I would like to ask you to approve the current government proposal. – Thank you very much.

There have simply been certain cases that were not covered by the original law. Having the citizenship of a state is incredibly valuable for each individual. Nevertheless, there are still so many people who are stateless, that is, who have no nationality. This is being fought by the state community. There are numerous conventions to combat statelessness, and this includes, in particular, the general prevention of statelessness. In principle, one acquires citizenship with birth. Whether to receive nationality due to the nationality of the parents or that of the state in which one is born depends on the respective law, but in principle one acquires nationality by birth. But if you do not have parents who grant you this right and do not come into existence in a state that gives you the right through birth, you have no citizenship, you cannot have one and is stateless from birth. That's exactly what this group is all about. It is about children who are born in Austria and whose parents are stateless. These are children who grow up here in Austria, who in Austria belong to the society, who go to kindergarten, to school, make an education – and yet do not belong to it. Not only do they not belong to the Austrian citizens, they have no other nationality, they do not belong to this group, to that group or to the group, they simply do not belong anywhere, and that all their youthful life. If, at the age of 18, they have the opportunity to apply for citizenship on their own behalf, then they have to know – as my previous speaker said – first of all. There is an incredible ignorance of this, not only of the persons concerned, but also of the relevant authorities, and as a state, as a society, I see ourselves in the responsibility to draw attention to this right. What we are doing here is a really small step, namely extending this period of time that young people have from the time of their maturity, when they start to make the driver's license, to get documents and to think at all about their rights and the possibility of naturalization. They therefore have a year's time to deal with all this in order to deal with the necessary authorities in order to make this request at all. From my point of view, this is the first small step that we have to take, and there are many more, because in principle it already starts with the fact that hardly a state around Austria is able to create a naturalisation under the age of 18 years. We also need to consider something, even there we need to look: How can we move forward? How can we prevent children who have no citizenship but who could have our citizenship because they are actually part of our society because they are actually a part of our society because they are growing up like children who have Austrian citizenship who have any citizenship at all. That's why it's such a big problem. Imagine that the children want to attend a school event where they have to cross a state border: The stateless children must stay at home. There are situations in the lives of these children in which they are kept in mind that they do not belong to them. We must fight this situation, we must change it. This is an important, very, very important step for those concerned, but also for Austria. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-03-23-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00147_d7e22190	Next speaker: Mr Christian Ries. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-03-23-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00147_d7e22215	Madam President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen of the House! Yes, the freedom of assembly is a great asset in every state that sees itself as democracy, and has been a capitalised right for every citizen in Austria since 1867. Of course, there must also be its limitations, even if a meeting threatens a higher-quality legal asset. A meeting can be forbidden – Mr Einwallner has already said it – if it is to be feared that criminal regulations will be violated – as we would now be back at the Loibach field – “or at risk of public security or public welfare”. According to the legal opinion in force, public security is expected to be at risk, for example, if life, freedom or human health is at risk. Therefore, if a forecast to be made shows that this is the case, a prohibition may enter into force as a preventive measure. This is already the law in force in the Convention. So you don't need this additional protection zone. Now, if we go away from a classical rally or assembly, we go to a rather loose connection, meeting people who have decided to distribute information sheets for example at a hospital driveway. There is a 1987 VfGH knowledge, which states that it is very likely to be against the use of the road for non-traffic purposes, because this action does not disproportionately restrict freedom of expression. So you also don't need this additional protection zone. Moreover, the SPG as such and also various national legislation already offer the possibility to intervene and to take directions if public order is threatened or the assistance is hindered or disturbed in public places. Yes, even the assignment of private property is possible if the presence seriously interferes with the rights of a owner, and this is undoubtedly the case in the blocking of a hospital entrance or in the disability of the hospital operation. So in this case too, we do not need this additional protection zone. That is also known by the applicants, otherwise they would not have provided this request with a Sunset clause. That is to say, at the end of the year this important issue falls again. Ladies and gentlemen, a functioning rule of law is characterised by the application of applicable law and by the fact that it is not arbitrarily stubbed in the legal system. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] Listen So you finally put on these show decisions that bear the character of empowerment laws!
